- [ ] **John Duns Scotus — Ordinatio**: Scotus (c. 1266-1308) was a Franciscan philosopher and theologian known for his doctrines of univocity of being, formal distinction, and haecceity (individual "thisness"). The Ordinatio is his major work, a revision of his lecture commentary on Peter Lombard's Sentences.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=John+Duns+Scotus+Ordinatio) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=John+Duns+Scotus+Ordinatio) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=John+Duns+Scotus+Ordinatio) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=John+Duns+Scotus+Ordinatio)
- [ ] **William of Ockham — Sum of Logic**: Ockham (c. 1287-1347) was an English Franciscan philosopher associated with nominalism, the position that universals do not exist independently of the mind. The Summa Logicae is a treatise on logic and philosophy of language; Ockham is also known for the methodological principle called Ockham's razor.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=William+of+Ockham+Sum+of+Logic) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=William+of+Ockham+Sum+of+Logic) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=William+of+Ockham+Sum+of+Logic) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=William+of+Ockham+Sum+of+Logic)
- [ ] **Ramon Llull — Ars Magna; Blanquerna**: Llull (c. 1232-1316) was a Catalan philosopher and missionary. The Ars Magna presents a combinatorial system intended to demonstrate religious truths logically; Blanquerna is a prose narrative about a man who becomes pope, written in Catalan.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Ramon+Llull+Ars+Magna) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Ramon+Llull+Ars+Magna) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Ramon+Llull+Ars+Magna) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Ramon+Llull+Ars+Magna)
- [ ] **Nicholas of Cusa — On Learned Ignorance**: Nicholas of Cusa (1401-1464) was a cardinal and philosopher. De Docta Ignorantia (1440) argues that human knowledge of the infinite God is necessarily limited and discusses the coincidence of opposites in the divine.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Nicholas+of+Cusa+On+Learned+Ignorance) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Nicholas+of+Cusa+On+Learned+Ignorance) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Nicholas+of+Cusa+On+Learned+Ignorance) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Nicholas+of+Cusa+On+Learned+Ignorance)
- [ ] **St. John Climacus — The Ladder of Divine Ascent**: A seventh-century Greek text by John, abbot of Mount Sinai, describing thirty steps of ascetic and spiritual progress. It is used in Eastern Orthodox monastic practice, particularly during Lent.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=John+Climacus+Ladder+of+Divine+Ascent) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=John+Climacus+Ladder+of+Divine+Ascent) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=John+Climacus+Ladder+of+Divine+Ascent) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=John+Climacus+Ladder+of+Divine+Ascent)
- [ ] **St. Symeon the New Theologian — The Discourses; Hymns of Divine Love**: Symeon (949-1022) was a Byzantine monk and mystical writer who wrote of direct experiential knowledge of God, including visions of divine light, attainable during life. His writings influenced later Orthodox spirituality, including Palamism.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Symeon+the+New+Theologian+Discourses) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Symeon+the+New+Theologian+Discourses) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Symeon+the+New+Theologian+Discourses) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Symeon+the+New+Theologian+Discourses)
- [ ] **St. Gregory Palamas — The Triads (In Defense of the Holy Hesychasts)**: A fourteenth-century Byzantine theologian, Palamas wrote in defense of hesychast monastic practice, including the Jesus Prayer. He formulated a distinction between God's essence, which is unknowable, and God's energies, through which humans experience divine grace.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Gregory+Palamas+Triads) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Gregory+Palamas+Triads) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Gregory+Palamas+Triads) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Gregory+Palamas+Triads)
- [ ] **Meister Eckhart — The Sermons**: Eckhart (c. 1260-1328) was a German Dominican theologian and mystic. His vernacular and Latin sermons discuss the soul's relationship to the Godhead using paradoxical and apophatic language; some propositions drawn from his teaching were condemned posthumously by Pope John XXII.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Meister+Eckhart+Sermons) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Meister+Eckhart+Sermons) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Meister+Eckhart+Sermons) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Meister+Eckhart+Sermons)
- [ ] **Johannes Tauler — The Sermons**: Tauler (c. 1300-1361) was a German Dominican preacher associated with Rhineland mysticism and a student of Eckhart's teaching. His sermons address detachment and the inner spiritual life in more practical terms than Eckhart's.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Johannes+Tauler+Sermons) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Johannes+Tauler+Sermons) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Johannes+Tauler+Sermons) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Johannes+Tauler+Sermons)
- [ ] **Jan van Ruusbroec — The Spiritual Espousals**: Ruusbroec (1293-1381) was a Flemish mystic. This work describes three stages of the spiritual life — active, interior, and contemplative — culminating in union with God, using the image of spiritual marriage.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Jan+van+Ruusbroec+Spiritual+Espousals) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Jan+van+Ruusbroec+Spiritual+Espousals) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Jan+van+Ruusbroec+Spiritual+Espousals) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Jan+van+Ruusbroec+Spiritual+Espousals)
- [ ] **St. Hildegard of Bingen — Scivias**: Hildegard (1098-1179) was a Benedictine abbess, composer, and writer. Scivias records a series of visions concerning creation, salvation history, and the Church, accompanied by theological commentary.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Hildegard+of+Bingen+Scivias) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Hildegard+of+Bingen+Scivias) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Hildegard+of+Bingen+Scivias) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Hildegard+of+Bingen+Scivias)
- [ ] **Richard Rolle — The Fire of Love**: Rolle (c. 1300-1349) was an English hermit and mystical writer. The Fire of Love (Incendium Amoris) describes his religious experiences in terms of physical sensations of heat and sweetness and the perception of song.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Richard+Rolle+Fire+of+Love) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Richard+Rolle+Fire+of+Love) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Richard+Rolle+Fire+of+Love) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Richard+Rolle+Fire+of+Love)
- [ ] **Walter Hilton — The Scale of Perfection**: Hilton (d. 1396) was an English Augustinian canon. This work describes the soul's progress from active religious observance toward contemplative union with God, addressed to a reader pursuing the contemplative life.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Walter+Hilton+Scale+of+Perfection) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Walter+Hilton+Scale+of+Perfection) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Walter+Hilton+Scale+of+Perfection) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Walter+Hilton+Scale+of+Perfection)
- [ ] **St. Catherine of Siena — The Dialogue**: Catherine (1347-1380) was an Italian Dominican tertiary involved in Church affairs, including efforts to return the papacy to Rome from Avignon. The Dialogue records a series of conversations between the soul and God, dictated by Catherine.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Catherine+of+Siena+Dialogue) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Catherine+of+Siena+Dialogue) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Catherine+of+Siena+Dialogue) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Catherine+of+Siena+Dialogue)
- [ ] **Boethius — The Theological Tractates (incl. De Trinitate)**: Short Latin treatises by Boethius applying logical analysis to questions concerning the Trinity and the person of Christ. They were widely studied in medieval schools alongside the Consolation of Philosophy. (Companion to the Consolation, Book I.)
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Boethius+Theological+Tractates) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Boethius+Theological+Tractates) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Boethius+Theological+Tractates) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Boethius+Theological+Tractates)
- [ ] **Henry Suso — The Little Book of Eternal Wisdom**: Suso (c. 1295-1366) was a German Dominican mystic and a student of Eckhart. This devotional work centers on meditation on the suffering of Christ and was widely circulated in the late medieval period.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Henry+Suso+Little+Book+of+Eternal+Wisdom) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Henry+Suso+Little+Book+of+Eternal+Wisdom) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Henry+Suso+Little+Book+of+Eternal+Wisdom) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Henry+Suso+Little+Book+of+Eternal+Wisdom)
- [ ] **Jean Gerson — On Mystical Theology**: Gerson (1363-1429) was chancellor of the University of Paris. His writings on mystical theology sought to distinguish contemplative experience from error and to address the subject within an academic and pastoral framework.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Jean+Gerson+On+Mystical+Theology) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Jean+Gerson+On+Mystical+Theology) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Jean+Gerson+On+Mystical+Theology) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Jean+Gerson+On+Mystical+Theology)
- [ ] **Joachim of Fiore — Exposition of the Apocalypse**: Joachim (c. 1135-1202) was a Calabrian abbot who proposed a theology of history divided into three ages, associated with the Father, the Son, and a coming age of the Spirit. His ideas influenced later apocalyptic and reform movements.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Joachim+of+Fiore+Exposition+of+the+Apocalypse) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Joachim+of+Fiore+Exposition+of+the+Apocalypse) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Joachim+of+Fiore+Exposition+of+the+Apocalypse) · [CCEL](https://www.ccel.org/search?qu=Joachim+of+Fiore+Exposition+of+the+Apocalypse)

<a id="phase-vi"></a>

## Phase VI: Judaism and Islam

*2nd to 20th c.. About 277 hours of core reading.*

The rabbinic tradition and the Islamic world: scripture, law, commentary, philosophy, and Persian and Arabic poetry. The medieval Arabic philosophers transmitted and extended Aristotle, which is why they sit next to Phase 5's scholastics. Maimonides belongs to both traditions here.

**Questions to carry through the phase**

1. How does rabbinic argument (dispute preserved, not resolved) compare with scholastic method?
2. What are the central themes of the Qur'an, and how is it organized?
3. What is at stake between al-Ghazali and Averroes on philosophy and revelation?
4. What does Sufi poetry mean by love, and how does it compare with Phase 5's mystics?

### Core sequence

1. [ ] **Pirkei Avot (The Ethics of the Fathers)** (~2 h)
   - Read: The ethical sayings of the rabbis and the chain of transmission from Sinai.
   - Edition: Koren Pirkei Avot (Koren); free on Sefaria
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Pirke+Aboth) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Pirkei+Avot) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Pirkei+Avot) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Pirkei+Avot) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Pirkei+Avot)
2. [ ] **The Mishnah** (~15 h)
   - Read: Selected tractates: Berakhot, Shabbat, Sanhedrin.
   - Edition: Tr. Herbert Danby, The Mishnah (Oxford); or Jacob Neusner (Yale); free on Sefaria
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Mishnah) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Mishnah) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Mishnah) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Mishnah)
3. [ ] **The Babylonian Talmud (Talmud Bavli)** (~20 h)
   - Read: Selections with a guide. Watch how a single Mishnah passage unfolds into layered debate.
   - Edition: Koren Talmud Bavli, Noe Edition (Koren); free on Sefaria (William Davidson edition). Guide: Barry Holtz, Back to the Sources (Simon & Schuster)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Babylonian+Talmud) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Babylonian+Talmud) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Babylonian+Talmud) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Babylonian+Talmud)
4. [ ] **The Midrash — Midrash Rabbah; Pirkei de-Rabbi Eliezer; Tanhuma** (~8 h)
   - Read: Genesis Rabbah read alongside Genesis.
   - Edition: Midrash Rabbah, Soncino translation; selections in Bialik and Ravnitzky, The Book of Legends (Schocken); free on Sefaria
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=The+Midrash+Midrash+Rabbah) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=The+Midrash+Midrash+Rabbah) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=The+Midrash+Midrash+Rabbah) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=The+Midrash+Midrash+Rabbah)
5. [ ] **Rashi — Commentary on the Torah and Talmud** (~10 h)
   - Read: Rashi on Genesis: the standard commentary printed beside the Torah text.
   - Edition: The Metsudah Linear Chumash with Rashi; free on Sefaria
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Rashi+Commentary+on+the+Torah+and+Talmud) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Rashi+Commentary+on+the+Torah+and+Talmud) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Rashi+Commentary+on+the+Torah+and+Talmud) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Rashi+Commentary+on+the+Torah+and+Talmud)
6. [ ] **The Qur'an** (~30 h)
   - Read: A translation with notes. One common approach: the short Meccan suras at the end first, then from the beginning.
   - Edition: Tr. M.A.S. Abdel Haleem (Oxford World's Classics); The Study Quran, ed. Seyyed Hossein Nasr (HarperOne); free at Quran.com
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Koran+Rodwell) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Qur%27an) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Qur%27an) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Qur%27an) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
7. [ ] **Ibn Ishaq / Ibn Hisham — The Life of Muhammad (Sirat Rasul Allah)** (~20 h)
   - Read: The earliest surviving biography of Muhammad, in Ibn Hisham's recension.
   - Edition: Tr. A. Guillaume, The Life of Muhammad (Oxford)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Ibn+Ishaq+%2F+Ibn+Hisham+Life+of+Muhammad) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Ibn+Ishaq+%2F+Ibn+Hisham+Life+of+Muhammad) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Ibn+Ishaq+%2F+Ibn+Hisham+Life+of+Muhammad) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
8. [ ] **Al-Nawawi — Forty Hadith** (~2 h)
   - Read: A compact entry into the hadith literature. Hadith 2 (Gabriel) summarizes the faith.
   - Edition: Tr. Ezzeddin Ibrahim and Denys Johnson-Davies (Islamic Texts Society)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Nawawi+Forty+Hadith) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Nawawi+Forty+Hadith) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Nawawi+Forty+Hadith) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
9. [ ] **The Mu'allaqat (The Seven Hanging Odes)** (~3 h)
   - Read: Pre-Islamic odes: the deserted campsite, the journey, the boast.
   - Edition: In A.J. Arberry, The Seven Odes (Allen & Unwin); or Michael Sells, Desert Tracings (Wesleyan)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Moallakat+Arabia) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Mu%27allaqat) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Mu%27allaqat) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Mu%27allaqat) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
10. [ ] **Al-Farabi — The Virtuous City** (~6 h)
   - Read: Plato's Republic reimagined with a philosopher-prophet as ruler.
   - Edition: Tr. Richard Walzer, Al-Farabi on the Perfect State (Oxford / Kazi)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Farabi+Virtuous+City) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Farabi+Virtuous+City) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Farabi+Virtuous+City) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
11. [ ] **Avicenna (Ibn Sina) — The Book of Healing; The Canon of Medicine** (~12 h)
   - Read: Selections: the metaphysics of necessary being and the 'floating man' argument from the Book of Healing.
   - Edition: The Metaphysics of The Healing, tr. Michael Marmura (Brigham Young); selections in Classical Arabic Philosophy, tr. McGinnis and Reisman (Hackett)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Avicenna+Book+of+Healing) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Avicenna+Book+of+Healing) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Avicenna+Book+of+Healing) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
12. [ ] **Al-Ghazali — The Incoherence of the Philosophers; The Revival of the Religious Sciences; Deliverance from Error** (~12 h)
   - Read: Deliverance from Error first (his autobiography), then the Incoherence of the Philosophers.
   - Edition: Deliverance from Error, tr. R.J. McCarthy (Fons Vitae); The Incoherence of the Philosophers, tr. Michael Marmura (Brigham Young)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Ghazali+Incoherence+of+the+Philosophers) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Ghazali+Incoherence+of+the+Philosophers) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Ghazali+Incoherence+of+the+Philosophers) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
13. [ ] **Averroes (Ibn Rushd) — The Incoherence of the Incoherence; The Decisive Treatise; the Aristotle commentaries** (~8 h)
   - Read: The Decisive Treatise, then selections from the Incoherence of the Incoherence answering al-Ghazali.
   - Edition: The Decisive Treatise, tr. Charles Butterworth (Brigham Young); Tahafut al-Tahafut tr. Simon van den Bergh (Gibb Memorial Trust)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Averroes+Incoherence+of+the+Incoherence) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Averroes+Incoherence+of+the+Incoherence) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Averroes+Incoherence+of+the+Incoherence) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
14. [ ] **Ibn Tufayl — Hayy ibn Yaqzan** (~4 h)
   - Read: A child raised alone on an island reasons his way to God. Often compared with Robinson Crusoe.
   - Edition: Tr. Lenn Evan Goodman, Hayy Ibn Yaqzan (Chicago)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Ibn+Tufayl+Hayy+ibn+Yaqzan) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Ibn+Tufayl+Hayy+ibn+Yaqzan) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Ibn+Tufayl+Hayy+ibn+Yaqzan) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
15. [ ] **Moses Maimonides — The Guide for the Perplexed** (~20 h)
   - Read: The introduction, Part I on divine attributes, and Part III on providence and the reasons for the commandments.
   - Edition: Tr. Shlomo Pines, The Guide of the Perplexed (Chicago, 2 vols.); abridged ed. Julius Guttmann (Hackett)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Maimonides+Guide+Perplexed+Friedlander) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Moses+Maimonides+Guide+for+the+Perplexed) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Moses+Maimonides+Guide+for+the+Perplexed) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Moses+Maimonides+Guide+for+the+Perplexed) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
16. [ ] **Judah Halevi — The Kuzari** (~8 h)
   - Read: A king questions a philosopher, a Christian, a Muslim, and a rabbi. A counterpoint to Maimonides.
   - Edition: Tr. Hartwig Hirschfeld, The Kuzari (Schocken); free on Sefaria
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Kuzari+Hirschfeld) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Judah+Halevi+Kuzari) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Judah+Halevi+Kuzari) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Judah+Halevi+Kuzari) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Judah+Halevi+Kuzari)
17. [ ] **Ferdowsi — The Shahnameh (The Book of Kings)** (~25 h)
   - Read: An abridged translation. The story of Rostam and Sohrab is the best-known episode.
   - Edition: Tr. Dick Davis, Shahnameh: The Persian Book of Kings (Penguin)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Ferdowsi+Shahnameh) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Ferdowsi+Shahnameh) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Ferdowsi+Shahnameh) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
18. [ ] **Attar — The Conference of the Birds** (~6 h)
   - Read: The birds' journey through seven valleys to the Simorgh.
   - Edition: Tr. Afkham Darbandi and Dick Davis (Penguin); or Sholeh Wolpe (Norton)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Attar+Conference+of+the+Birds) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Attar+Conference+of+the+Birds) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Attar+Conference+of+the+Birds) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
19. [ ] **Rumi — The Masnavi; Diwan-e Shams-e Tabrizi; Fihi Ma Fihi** (~12 h)
   - Read: Masnavi Book I, beginning with the song of the reed.
   - Edition: The Masnavi, Book One, tr. Jawid Mojaddedi (Oxford World's Classics); ghazals tr. A.J. Arberry, Mystical Poems of Rumi (Chicago)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Rumi+Masnavi+Whinfield) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Rumi+Masnavi) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Rumi+Masnavi) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Rumi+Masnavi) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
20. [ ] **Saadi — The Gulistan; The Bustan** (~5 h)
   - Read: The Gulistan: prose and verse anecdotes of conduct.
   - Edition: The Gulistan tr. Wheeler Thackston (Ibex)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Gulistan+Sadi) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Saadi+Gulistan) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Saadi+Gulistan) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Saadi+Gulistan) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
21. [ ] **Hafiz — The Divan** (~5 h)
   - Read: A selection of ghazals. Goethe's West-Eastern Divan (not on this list) was written in response to him.
   - Edition: Tr. Dick Davis, Faces of Love: Hafez and the Poets of Shiraz (Penguin)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Hafiz+Divan) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Hafiz+Divan) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Hafiz+Divan) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
22. [ ] **Anon. — The Thousand and One Nights** (~20 h)
   - Read: The frame tale of Shahrazad and a selection; note the nested stories.
   - Edition: Tr. Husain Haddawy, The Arabian Nights (Norton); or Malcolm and Ursula Lyons (Penguin, 3 vols.)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Arabian+Nights+Lane) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Thousand+and+One+Nights) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Thousand+and+One+Nights) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Thousand+and+One+Nights) · [Chinese Text Project](https://ctext.org/)
23. [ ] **Ibn Khaldun — The Muqaddimah** (~20 h)
   - Read: The theory of asabiyya (group solidarity) and the rise and fall of dynasties.
   - Edition: Tr. Franz Rosenthal, abridged by N.J. Dawood, The Muqaddimah (Princeton)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Ibn+Khaldun+Muqaddimah) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Ibn+Khaldun+Muqaddimah) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Ibn+Khaldun+Muqaddimah) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
24. [ ] **Martin Buber — I and Thou** (~4 h)
   - Read: A modern bridge: the I-Thou and I-It relations.
   - Edition: Tr. Walter Kaufmann, I and Thou (Scribner / Touchstone)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Martin+Buber+I+and+Thou) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Martin+Buber+I+and+Thou) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Martin+Buber+I+and+Thou) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Martin+Buber+I+and+Thou)

**Synthesis.** Set Maimonides, Averroes, and Aquinas side by side on the relation of reason and revelation.

### Deeper shelf

- [ ] **The Tosefta**: A companion collection of tannaitic teachings organized parallel to the Mishnah's structure, containing material not included in the Mishnah, including additional rulings and discussions.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Tosefta) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Tosefta) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Tosefta) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Tosefta)
- [ ] **The Jerusalem Talmud (Talmud Yerushalmi)**: Compiled in the Land of Israel and completed earlier than the Babylonian Talmud, around the 4th-5th century AD. It comments on the Mishnah and preserves traditions and rulings distinct from those in the Babylonian Talmud.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Jerusalem+Talmud) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Jerusalem+Talmud) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Jerusalem+Talmud) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Jerusalem+Talmud)
- [ ] **The Targums — Onkelos; Jonathan**: Aramaic translations of the Hebrew Bible, produced for congregational and study use after Aramaic replaced Hebrew as the everyday language of many Jewish communities. They combine translation with interpretive additions.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=The+Targums+Onkelos) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=The+Targums+Onkelos) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=The+Targums+Onkelos) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=The+Targums+Onkelos)
- [ ] **Moses Maimonides — The Mishneh Torah; the Thirteen Principles of Faith**: Maimonides (1138-1204) was a rabbi, philosopher, and physician. The Mishneh Torah is a comprehensive code organizing Jewish law by topic; the Thirteen Principles summarize core tenets of Jewish belief, formulated in his commentary on the Mishnah.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Moses+Maimonides+Mishneh+Torah) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Moses+Maimonides+Mishneh+Torah) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Moses+Maimonides+Mishneh+Torah) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Moses+Maimonides+Mishneh+Torah)
- [ ] **Joseph Karo — The Shulchan Aruch**: A sixteenth-century code of Jewish law compiled by Joseph Karo, organized by topic and covering daily religious practice, festivals, and personal and civil law. It became a standard legal reference, supplemented by later glosses reflecting Ashkenazi custom.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Joseph+Karo+Shulchan+Aruch) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Joseph+Karo+Shulchan+Aruch) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Joseph+Karo+Shulchan+Aruch) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Joseph+Karo+Shulchan+Aruch)
- [ ] **Saadia Gaon — The Book of Beliefs and Opinions**: Saadia Gaon (882-942), head of the Sura academy in Babylonia, wrote this work of Jewish philosophy in Arabic, addressing the relationship between reason and revelation and defending Jewish doctrine using philosophical argument.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Saadia+Gaon+Book+of+Beliefs+and+Opinions) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Saadia+Gaon+Book+of+Beliefs+and+Opinions) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Saadia+Gaon+Book+of+Beliefs+and+Opinions) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Saadia+Gaon+Book+of+Beliefs+and+Opinions)
- [ ] **Bahya ibn Paquda — Duties of the Heart**: An eleventh-century work of Jewish ethics, written in Arabic, distinguishing between the "duties of the limbs" (external observance) and the "duties of the heart" (internal states such as humility, trust, and sincerity).
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Bahya+ibn+Paquda+Duties+of+the+Heart) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Bahya+ibn+Paquda+Duties+of+the+Heart) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Bahya+ibn+Paquda+Duties+of+the+Heart) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Bahya+ibn+Paquda+Duties+of+the+Heart)
- [ ] **Sefer ha-Bahir (The Book of Brightness)**: An early Kabbalistic text, appearing in the twelfth century, presenting concepts including the sefirot (divine emanations) and the transmigration of souls in a cryptic, symbolic style.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Sefer+ha-Bahir) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Sefer+ha-Bahir) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Sefer+ha-Bahir) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Sefer+ha-Bahir)
- [ ] **Abraham Abulafia — The Light of the Intellect**: Abulafia (1240-c. 1291) developed a form of Kabbalah involving meditative techniques based on combinations of Hebrew letters and divine names, aimed at inducing prophetic or ecstatic states.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Abraham+Abulafia+Light+of+the+Intellect) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Abraham+Abulafia+Light+of+the+Intellect) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Abraham+Abulafia+Light+of+the+Intellect) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Abraham+Abulafia+Light+of+the+Intellect)
- [ ] **Joseph Gikatilla — Gates of Light**: A thirteenth-century Kabbalistic text by Joseph Gikatilla explaining the ten sefirot and their associated divine names, intended as an introductory guide to Kabbalistic symbolism.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Joseph+Gikatilla+Gates+of+Light) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Joseph+Gikatilla+Gates+of+Light) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Joseph+Gikatilla+Gates+of+Light) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Joseph+Gikatilla+Gates+of+Light)
- [ ] **Moses Cordovero — Pardes Rimonim**: A sixteenth-century systematic treatise by the Safed Kabbalist Moses Cordovero, organizing earlier Kabbalistic teachings, particularly concerning the sefirot, into a structured theological framework.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Moses+Cordovero+Pardes+Rimonim) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Moses+Cordovero+Pardes+Rimonim) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Moses+Cordovero+Pardes+Rimonim) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Moses+Cordovero+Pardes+Rimonim)
- [ ] **Isaac Luria & Chaim Vital — Etz Chaim (The Tree of Life)**: Records the teachings of Isaac Luria (1534-1572), a Kabbalist active in Safed, as compiled by his student Chaim Vital. Central concepts include tzimtzum (divine self-contraction), the breaking of the vessels, and tikkun (repair), describing the process of creation and its restoration.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Isaac+Luria+%26+Chaim+Vital+Etz+Chaim) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Isaac+Luria+%26+Chaim+Vital+Etz+Chaim) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Isaac+Luria+%26+Chaim+Vital+Etz+Chaim) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Isaac+Luria+%26+Chaim+Vital+Etz+Chaim)
- [ ] **Moses Chaim Luzzatto — The Path of the Just (Mesillat Yesharim)**: An eighteenth-century Italian work of Jewish ethics describing stages of moral and spiritual development, from care in observance to piety and holiness, drawing on earlier rabbinic sources including Pirkei Avot.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Moses+Chaim+Luzzatto+Path+of+the+Just) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Moses+Chaim+Luzzatto+Path+of+the+Just) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Moses+Chaim+Luzzatto+Path+of+the+Just) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Moses+Chaim+Luzzatto+Path+of+the+Just)
- [ ] **Sefer Raziel HaMalakh**: A compilation of Kabbalistic, angelological, and magical material, traditionally attributed to the angel Raziel, including material on amulets and cosmology, circulated from the medieval period onward.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Sefer+Raziel+HaMalakh) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Sefer+Raziel+HaMalakh) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Sefer+Raziel+HaMalakh) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Sefer+Raziel+HaMalakh)
- [ ] **The Passover Haggadah**: The text recited during the Passover seder, recounting the biblical account of the Exodus from Egypt and structuring the ritual meal, including prescribed blessings, questions, and readings.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Passover+Haggadah) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Passover+Haggadah) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Passover+Haggadah) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Passover+Haggadah)
- [ ] **Nachman of Breslov — Likutei Moharan; The Tales**: Nachman of Breslov (1772-1810) was a Hasidic rabbi. Likutei Moharan collects his teachings and discourses; his tales are a series of allegorical stories addressing themes of faith and struggle, recorded by his followers.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Nachman+of+Breslov+Likutei+Moharan) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Nachman+of+Breslov+Likutei+Moharan) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Nachman+of+Breslov+Likutei+Moharan) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Nachman+of+Breslov+Likutei+Moharan)
- [ ] **Abraham Joshua Heschel — The Sabbath; God in Search of Man**: Heschel (1907-1972) was a rabbi and theologian. The Sabbath discusses the Jewish observance of the Sabbath as a structuring of time; God in Search of Man presents his approach to Jewish theology and the concept of divine pathos.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Abraham+Joshua+Heschel+Sabbath) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Abraham+Joshua+Heschel+Sabbath) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Abraham+Joshua+Heschel+Sabbath) · [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org/search?q=Abraham+Joshua+Heschel+Sabbath)
- [ ] **Antarah ibn Shaddad — Diwan; Sirat Antar**: Antarah was a pre-Islamic poet-warrior, son of an Arab father and an enslaved Ethiopian mother, known for verses on love and battle addressed to his beloved Abla. The Sirat Antar is a later popular romance elaborating his life into an extended chivalric narrative.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Antarah+ibn+Shaddad+Diwan) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Antarah+ibn+Shaddad+Diwan) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Antarah+ibn+Shaddad+Diwan) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Abu Nuwas — Diwan**: An Abbasid-era poet (c. 756-814) known for verse on wine and love, as well as satire and hunting poems, associated with the court culture of Baghdad. His work departed from the conventions of earlier Arabic poetry.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Abu+Nuwas+Diwan) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Abu+Nuwas+Diwan) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Abu+Nuwas+Diwan) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Al-Mutanabbi — Diwan**: A tenth-century Arabic poet (915-965) known for panegyric and self-praise poetry composed for various rulers and patrons. His verse is frequently cited in later Arabic literary and rhetorical tradition.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Mutanabbi+Diwan) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Mutanabbi+Diwan) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Mutanabbi+Diwan) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Abu al-Ala al-Maarri — The Epistle of Forgiveness; Luzumiyyat**: Al-Maarri (973-1057) was a blind Syrian poet and writer. The Epistle of Forgiveness depicts a journey through the afterlife and has been compared to Dante's Divine Comedy; the Luzumiyyat is a poetry collection expressing skeptical and ascetic views, including criticism of organized religion.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Abu+al-Ala+al-Maarri+Epistle+of+Forgiveness) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Abu+al-Ala+al-Maarri+Epistle+of+Forgiveness) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Abu+al-Ala+al-Maarri+Epistle+of+Forgiveness) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Ibn al-Muqaffa — Kalila wa Dimna**: An eighth-century Arabic adaptation, by Ibn al-Muqaffa, of Indian animal fables originating in the Sanskrit Panchatantra, concerning statecraft and ethics, told through the interactions of animal characters. It was translated into numerous languages in subsequent centuries.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Ibn+al-Muqaffa+Kalila+wa+Dimna) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Ibn+al-Muqaffa+Kalila+wa+Dimna) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Ibn+al-Muqaffa+Kalila+wa+Dimna) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Al-Hariri — The Maqamat**: A collection of fifty narrative episodes composed by al-Hariri of Basra (1054-1122) in rhymed prose (saj') featuring a recurring eloquent and roguish protagonist. The maqamat form emphasizes linguistic virtuosity and wordplay.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Hariri+Maqamat) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Hariri+Maqamat) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Hariri+Maqamat) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Usama ibn Munqidh — The Book of Contemplation**: A memoir by a twelfth-century Syrian nobleman and warrior describing his experiences during the Crusades, including encounters with Frankish crusaders, alongside accounts of hunting and court life.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Usama+ibn+Munqidh+Book+of+Contemplation) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Usama+ibn+Munqidh+Book+of+Contemplation) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Usama+ibn+Munqidh+Book+of+Contemplation) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Sahih al-Bukhari (a selection)**: A ninth-century collection of hadith (reports of the sayings and actions of Muhammad) compiled by Muhammad al-Bukhari, organized by subject and evaluated for the reliability of their chains of transmission (isnad). It is regarded by Sunni Muslims as one of the most authoritative hadith collections.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Sahih+al-Bukhari) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Sahih+al-Bukhari) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Sahih+al-Bukhari) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Sahih Muslim**: A hadith collection compiled by Muslim ibn al-Hajjaj in the ninth century, regarded alongside Sahih al-Bukhari as one of the two collections given the highest degree of authority in Sunni Islam.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Sahih+Muslim) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Sahih+Muslim) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Sahih+Muslim) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Al-Tabari — History of the Prophets and Kings**: A universal history by the ninth/tenth-century scholar al-Tabari, covering events from creation through the author's own time, including pre-Islamic history and the early Islamic period, compiled from earlier sources and oral traditions.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Tabari+History+of+the+Prophets+and+Kings) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Tabari+History+of+the+Prophets+and+Kings) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Tabari+History+of+the+Prophets+and+Kings) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Rudaki — Diwan**: Rudaki (c. 858-941) is regarded as one of the earliest major poets writing in New Persian, active at the Samanid court in Bukhara. Only fragments of his extensive body of work survive.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Rudaki+Diwan) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Rudaki+Diwan) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Rudaki+Diwan) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Sanai — The Walled Garden of Truth**: A twelfth-century Persian poem by Sanai of Ghazna, generally considered among the earliest major works to use the Persian masnavi (rhyming couplet) form for extended Sufi religious instruction, combining doctrine, parable, and homily.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Sanai+Walled+Garden+of+Truth) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Sanai+Walled+Garden+of+Truth) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Sanai+Walled+Garden+of+Truth) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Nizami Ganjavi — The Khamsa (Layla and Majnun; Khosrow and Shirin; The Seven Beauties)**: A collection of five narrative poems composed in the twelfth century by Nizami, including Layla and Majnun (the story of ill-fated lovers), Khosrow and Shirin, and The Seven Beauties (Haft Paykar). The poems combine romance with historical and legendary material.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Nizami+Ganjavi+Khamsa+%28Layla+and+Majnun) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Nizami+Ganjavi+Khamsa+%28Layla+and+Majnun) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Nizami+Ganjavi+Khamsa+%28Layla+and+Majnun) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Jami — Yusuf and Zulaikha; Haft Awrang**: Jami (1414-1492) was a Persian poet and Sufi scholar, often regarded as among the last of the major classical Persian poets. Yusuf and Zulaikha retells the biblical/Quranic story of Joseph as a Sufi allegory of love; Haft Awrang ("Seven Thrones") is a collection of seven narrative poems.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Jami+Yusuf+and+Zulaikha) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Jami+Yusuf+and+Zulaikha) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Jami+Yusuf+and+Zulaikha) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Al-Kindi — On First Philosophy**: Al-Kindi (c. 801-873), known as "the philosopher of the Arabs," was among the first to systematically incorporate Greek philosophical concepts into Arabic-language Islamic thought. On First Philosophy addresses metaphysics and argues for the compatibility of philosophy with Islamic doctrine.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Kindi+On+First+Philosophy) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Kindi+On+First+Philosophy) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Kindi+On+First+Philosophy) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Suhrawardi — The Philosophy of Illumination**: Shihab al-Din Suhrawardi (1154-1191) founded the Illuminationist (Ishraqi) school of Islamic philosophy, presenting a metaphysics organized around degrees of light emanating from a "Light of Lights," drawing on Platonic and Zoroastrian sources. He was executed in Aleppo.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Suhrawardi+Philosophy+of+Illumination) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Suhrawardi+Philosophy+of+Illumination) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Suhrawardi+Philosophy+of+Illumination) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Ibn Arabi — The Bezels of Wisdom; The Meccan Revelations**: Ibn Arabi (1165-1240) was an Andalusian Sufi philosopher known for the doctrine of wahdat al-wujud (the unity of being). The Bezels of Wisdom (Fusus al-Hikam) discusses the spiritual character of successive prophets; The Meccan Revelations (al-Futuhat al-Makkiyya) is an extensive compendium of his mystical and philosophical teachings.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Ibn+Arabi+Bezels+of+Wisdom) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Ibn+Arabi+Bezels+of+Wisdom) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Ibn+Arabi+Bezels+of+Wisdom) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Omar Khayyam — Treatise on Algebra**: Omar Khayyam (1048-1131), better known in the West for his quatrains (the Rubaiyat), was also a mathematician and astronomer. His algebraic treatise classifies and provides geometric solutions to cubic equations; he also contributed to the reform of the Persian solar calendar.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Omar+Khayyam+Treatise+on+Algebra) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Omar+Khayyam+Treatise+on+Algebra) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Omar+Khayyam+Treatise+on+Algebra) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Al-Jahiz — The Book of Animals; The Book of Misers**: Al-Jahiz (c. 776-868) was an Abbasid-era prose writer. The Book of Animals is a wide-ranging compilation combining zoological material with anecdote and digression; The Book of Misers collects satirical anecdotes about miserly behavior.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Jahiz+Book+of+Animals) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Jahiz+Book+of+Animals) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Jahiz+Book+of+Animals) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Al-Masudi — The Meadows of Gold**: Al-Masudi (c. 896-956) was a historian and geographer. The Meadows of Gold is a wide-ranging work combining history, geography, and anecdote drawn from his travels and reading, covering regions from the Islamic world to India and beyond.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Masudi+Meadows+of+Gold) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Masudi+Meadows+of+Gold) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Masudi+Meadows+of+Gold) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Rabia al-Adawiyya — Poems and Sayings**: Rabia al-Adawiyya (c. 717-801) was an early female Sufi figure from Basra, associated with the teaching that God should be loved for God's own sake, without hope of reward or fear of punishment. Her sayings survive primarily through later biographical and hagiographical sources.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Rabia+al-Adawiyya+Poems+and+Sayings) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Rabia+al-Adawiyya+Poems+and+Sayings) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Rabia+al-Adawiyya+Poems+and+Sayings) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Al-Hallaj — The Tawasin; the poems**: Al-Hallaj (858-922) was a Sufi mystic executed in Baghdad, reportedly in part for the statement "ana al-Haqq" ("I am the Truth"), interpreted as a claim of mystical union with God. The Tawasin is a collection of his prose and poetic writings.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Al-Hallaj+Tawasin) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Al-Hallaj+Tawasin) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Al-Hallaj+Tawasin) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)
- [ ] **Nasir Khusraw — Diwan; Safarnama**: Nasir Khusraw (1004-1088) was a Persian poet, philosopher, and Ismaili missionary. The Safarnama recounts a seven-year journey he made to Mecca, Cairo, and other regions; his Diwan addresses ethical and religious themes.
  - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Nasir+Khusraw+Diwan) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Nasir+Khusraw+Diwan) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Nasir+Khusraw+Diwan) · [Quran.com](https://quran.com/)

<a id="phase-vii"></a>

## Phase VII: India, China, and Japan

*c. 1500 BC to 18th c.. About 257 hours of core reading.*

Three linked traditions. India first (Veda, Upanishads, epics, early Buddhism), then China (Confucian and Daoist classics, poetry), then Buddhism's development through Madhyamaka and Chan into Japanese Zen and the warrior literature. The later sections of this phase assume the earlier ones.

**Questions to carry through the phase**

1. What do the Upanishads mean by atman and brahman, and how does the Buddha's teaching of anatta respond?
2. What does Krishna tell Arjuna about action and its fruits?
3. Where do Confucius and Laozi agree, and where do they part?
4. How does Zen's emphasis on direct experience relate to the sutras it inherited?

### Core sequence

1. [ ] **The Rigveda (the Vedic hymns)** (~8 h)
   - Read: Selected hymns, including the Creation Hymn (X.129) and the Purusha hymn (X.90).
   - Edition: Tr. Wendy Doniger, The Rig Veda: An Anthology (Penguin)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Rig+Veda+Griffith) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Rigveda) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Rigveda) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Rigveda)
2. [ ] **The Principal Upanishads — Isha, Katha, Kena, Mundaka, Chandogya, Brihadaranyaka** (~10 h)
   - Read: Isha and Katha first (short), then Chandogya VI (tat tvam asi) and Brihadaranyaka.
   - Edition: Tr. Patrick Olivelle, Upanisads (Oxford World's Classics); or Eknath Easwaran (Nilgiri)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Upanishads+Max+Muller) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=The+Principal+Upanishads+Isha%2C+Katha%2C+Kena%2C+Mundaka%2C+Chandogya%2C+Brihadaranyaka) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=The+Principal+Upanishads+Isha%2C+Katha%2C+Kena%2C+Mundaka%2C+Chandogya%2C+Brihadaranyaka) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=The+Principal+Upanishads+Isha%2C+Katha%2C+Kena%2C+Mundaka%2C+Chandogya%2C+Brihadaranyaka)
3. [ ] **The Bhagavad Gita** (~5 h)
   - Read: Read twice: once for the story, once for the three yogas of action, knowledge, and devotion.
   - Edition: Tr. Laurie Patton (Penguin) or Eknath Easwaran (Nilgiri)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Bhagavad+Gita+Arnold) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Bhagavad+Gita) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Bhagavad+Gita) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Bhagavad+Gita) · [Chinese Text Project](https://ctext.org/)
4. [ ] **Valmiki — The Ramayana** (~20 h)
   - Read: An abridged translation is standard for a first reading.
   - Edition: Abridged tr. Arshia Sattar (Penguin); or R.K. Narayan's prose retelling (Penguin)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Ramayana+Griffith) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Valmiki+Ramayana) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Valmiki+Ramayana) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Valmiki+Ramayana) · [Chinese Text Project](https://ctext.org/)
5. [ ] **Vyasa (attrib.) — The Mahabharata** (~40 h)
   - Read: An abridgment. The Gita sits inside it; now read it in context.
   - Edition: Abridged tr. John D. Smith (Penguin); or C. Rajagopalachari's retelling (Bharatiya Vidya Bhavan)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Mahabharata+Ganguli) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Vyasa+Mahabharata) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Vyasa+Mahabharata) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Vyasa+Mahabharata) · [Chinese Text Project](https://ctext.org/)
6. [ ] **The Pali Canon (Tipitaka) — the Digha and Majjhima Nikayas** (~20 h)
   - Read: Digha Nikaya 2 and 16 (the Buddha's last days); Majjhima Nikaya 22 (the raft) and 63 (the poisoned arrow).
   - Edition: Tr. Bhikkhu Nanamoli and Bhikkhu Bodhi, The Middle Length Discourses (Wisdom); Maurice Walshe, The Long Discourses (Wisdom); Bhikkhu Bodhi, In the Buddha's Words (Wisdom) as an anthology; free at SuttaCentral
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=The+Pali+Canon+Digha+and+Majjhima+Nikayas) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=The+Pali+Canon+Digha+and+Majjhima+Nikayas) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=The+Pali+Canon+Digha+and+Majjhima+Nikayas) · [SuttaCentral](https://www.suttacentral.net/search?query=The+Pali+Canon+Digha+and+Majjhima+Nikayas)
7. [ ] **The Dhammapada** (~3 h)
   - Read: Short verses on the Theravada path.
   - Edition: Tr. Gil Fronsdal (Shambhala) or Valerie Roebuck (Penguin)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Dhammapada+Muller) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Dhammapada) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Dhammapada) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Dhammapada) · [Chinese Text Project](https://ctext.org/)
8. [ ] **Patanjali — The Yoga Sutras** (~4 h)
   - Read: The eight limbs, with a commentary.
   - Edition: Tr. Edwin F. Bryant, The Yoga Sutras of Patanjali (North Point)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Yoga+Sutras+Patanjali) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Patanjali+Yoga+Sutras) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Patanjali+Yoga+Sutras) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Patanjali+Yoga+Sutras)
9. [ ] **Nagarjuna — The Fundamental Verses on the Middle Way** (~6 h)
   - Read: Chapters 1, 18, 24, 25 with a commentary. Emptiness and the two truths.
   - Edition: Tr. Jay L. Garfield, The Fundamental Wisdom of the Middle Way (Oxford)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Nagarjuna+Fundamental+Verses+on+the+Middle+Way) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Nagarjuna+Fundamental+Verses+on+the+Middle+Way) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Nagarjuna+Fundamental+Verses+on+the+Middle+Way) · [SuttaCentral](https://www.suttacentral.net/search?query=Nagarjuna+Fundamental+Verses+on+the+Middle+Way)
10. [ ] **The Heart Sutra** (~1 h)
   - Read: One page: 'form is emptiness, emptiness is form.'
   - Edition: Red Pine, The Heart Sutra (Counterpoint); or in Edward Conze, Buddhist Wisdom Books (Allen & Unwin)
   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Heart+Sutra) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Heart+Sutra) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Heart+Sutra) · [SuttaCentral](https://www.suttacentral.net/search?query=Heart+Sutra)
11. [ ] **The Diamond Sutra** (~2 h)
   - Read: Short. The oldest dated printed book (868) is a copy of it.
   - Edition: Red Pine, The Diamond Sutra (Counterpoint)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Diamond+Sutra) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Diamond+Sutra) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Diamond+Sutra) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Diamond+Sutra) · [SuttaCentral](https://www.suttacentral.net/search?query=Diamond+Sutra)
12. [ ] **Shantideva — The Way of the Bodhisattva** (~8 h)
   - Read: Chapter 6 (patience) and chapter 8 (exchanging self and other).
   - Edition: Tr. Padmakara Translation Group, The Way of the Bodhisattva (Shambhala); or Crosby and Skilton, The Bodhicaryavatara (Oxford World's Classics)

13. [ ] **Confucius — The Analects** (~5 h)
   - Read: Ren (humaneness), li (ritual), the junzi (gentleman).
   - Edition: Tr. Edward Slingerland (Hackett) or D.C. Lau (Penguin); free at Chinese Text Project (Legge)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Analects+Confucius+Legge) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Confucius+Analects) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Confucius+Analects) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Confucius+Analects) · [Chinese Text Project](https://ctext.org/)
14. [ ] **Mencius — The Mencius** (~6 h)
   - Read: The four sprouts (2A6) and the claim that human nature is good.
   - Edition: Tr. Bryan Van Norden (Hackett) or D.C. Lau (Penguin); free at Chinese Text Project (Legge)
   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Mencius+Legge) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Mencius+Mencius) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Mencius+Mencius) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Mencius+Mencius) · [Chinese Text Project](https://ctext.org/)
15. [ ] **Laozi — Tao Te Ching** (~3 h)
   - Read: Compare two translations; they diverge widely.

16. [ ] **Zhuangzi — The Zhuangzi** (~8 h)
   - Read: The Inner Chapters (1-7): the butterfly dream, Cook Ding.

17. [ ] **Sun Tzu — The Art of War** (~3 h)
   - Read: Short. Winning without fighting; knowing yourself and the enemy.

18. [ ] **The I Ching (Book of Changes)** (~5 h)
   - Read: The structure of the 64 hexagrams and the Great Commentary (Xici).

19. [ ] **The Shijing (The Book of Songs)** (~4 h)
   - Read: A selection; Confucius is said to have compiled it.

   - Find it: [Free public-domain text (Gutenberg search)](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Shi+King+Legge) · [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Shijing) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Shijing) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Shijing) · [Chinese Text Project](https://ctext.org/)
20. [ ] **Du Fu (Tu Fu) — Poems** (~3 h)
   - Read: With Li Bai, the two poets of the high Tang. Du Fu writes war and displacement.

   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Du+Fu+Poems) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Du+Fu+Poems) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Du+Fu+Poems) · [Chinese Text Project](https://ctext.org/)

   - Read: The poetry contest for the patriarchate and the teaching of sudden awakening.

   - Read: Short essays on impermanence and taste.

   - Read: Letters to a swordsman on the mind that does not stop.

   - Find it: [Open Library](https://openlibrary.org/search?q=Takuan+Soho+Unfettered+Mind) · [Internet Archive](https://archive.org/search?query=Takuan+Soho+Unfettered+Mind) · [Project Gutenberg](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/search/?query=Takuan+Soho+Unfettered+Mind) · [SuttaCentral](https://www.suttacentral.net/search?query=Takuan+Soho+Unfettered+Mind)

   - Read: The Water and Void books.

   - Read: A selection; it was compiled from conversations c. 1716.
